MathEngine Upgrade Audit

Upgrade audit for improving the existing engine math stack in place into a shared pure-JavaScript MathEngine layer. This is not a new engine plan. It keeps current bootstraps and import paths stable while adding missing math, tests, metadata, and CPU/WGSL parity.

Audit date: 2026-07-18 Repo: C:\Coding\game Current source: engine/core/math Upgrade target: in-place shared MathEngine layer Secondary systems: camera, physics, shaders, packages, media, network Result: Gate 0 and Gate 1 closed; Gate 4 parity and compile coverage are partially enforced

Upgrade Decision

Gate 0 Pass Existing Math Hardened

Latest slice: Model Morph Weight Editor State Upgrade (implemented): engine/core/math/MeshAttributeMath.js now owns strict serializable [{partIndex,mode,weights}] admission using stable imported part indexes, exact target counts, unique ownership, 256-target capacity, and the existing glTF-compatible +/-10,000 weight bound. The existing engine/render/mesh/SkeletalAnimation.js remains the sole morph and skinning runtime: it exposes model descriptor/get/set APIs, keeps authored base weights separate from manual controls, prevents animation ticks from overwriting manual mode, restores animation ownership explicitly, and resets clips without weight channels to authored defaults. engine/tools/inspector/entityEditors/ModelMorphEditor.js adds named-target sliders, exact numeric fields, animated/manual mode selection, and reset-to-animation controls through the mounted Editor inspector. ModelImporter.js, SceneManager.js, EditorHistory.js, EditorApp.js, CollabSceneSync.js, and EditorCollab.js preserve and validate the same control state across import, scene save/load, duplication, undo/redo, collaboration operations, and snapshots; invalid persisted state falls back to authored target weights and canonical runtime metadata. Invariant 531 covers pure bounds, stable part mapping, named descriptors, manual survival across ticks, animation resumption, authored-default restoration, invalid-state rejection without mutation, collaboration round trips, persistence ownership, and inspector wiring. The harness now contains 322 runTest blocks and 2,288 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-18.model-morph-weight-editor-state-v1 with 655 valid entries. Current remaining gaps: GPU-compute morph evaluation, KHR_animation_pointer, animation blending/cross-fades, clip events/markers, captured Khronos ordinary-node/CUBICSPLINE and AnimatedMorphCube fixtures, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures. Previous Model Animation Playback slice: engine/core/math/AnimationTimeMath.js now owns strict serializable {clipIndex,time,speed,playing,loop} admission with actual clip-duration and +/-16x speed bounds. The existing engine/render/mesh/SkeletalAnimation.js remains the sole playback engine and now exposes descriptor/get/set APIs for every imported clip, including weight-only clips; no replacement engine or second clock was added. engine/tools/inspector/entityEditors/ModelAnimationEditor.js adds the mounted Editor clip selector, play/pause, restart, time scrubber, speed field, and loop toggle through EditorApp.js. ModelImporter.js, SceneManager.js, EditorHistory.js, Viewport.js, CollabSceneSync.js, and EditorCollab.js preserve and validate the same state across import, scene save/load, duplication, undo/redo, live clock advancement, collaboration operations, and snapshots. Invalid persisted state falls back to clip defaults without preventing model registration. Invariant 530 covers bounds, weight-only clip visibility, runtime mutation/rejection, history/source ownership, collaboration operation and snapshot round trips, viewport persistence, and inspector wiring. The harness now contains 321 runTest blocks and 2,287 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-17.model-animation-playback-state-v1 with 654 valid entries. Still missing: morph-weight Editor controls and persistence, GPU-compute morph evaluation, KHR_animation_pointer, animation blending/cross-fades, clip events/markers, captured Khronos ordinary-node/CUBICSPLINE fixtures, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures. Previous Ordinary glTF Node Animation slice: editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js now extracts and validates the active glTF node graph alongside strict animation channels: target nodes and paths, unique node/path ownership, nonnegative strictly increasing key times, finite outputs, interpolation modes, and TRS output widths are admitted before runtime registration, while animated nodes authored with matrix fail closed as required by glTF. The existing engine/render/mesh/SkeletalAnimation.js owns ordinary-node playback through one ModelNodeAnimationInstance per persisted modelInstanceId; each instance resets from authored TRS, samples LINEAR/STEP/CUBICSPLINE channels through shared AnimationTimeMath.animationSampleChannelInto(), normalizes animated quaternions, composes parent-first T*R*S world matrices, supplies mesh-relative joint matrices plus the animated mesh-node transform to existing skinning, and keeps duplicate entities on independent clocks. ViewportMeshUtils.js, Viewport.js, and EditorApp.js resolve the same transient matrix for main rendering, shadows, frustum bounds, spatial-index refresh, camera focus, and raycast. Static meshParts.nodeWorldMatrix remains the serialized fallback; SceneManager and history reload re-register from the source asset, collaboration continues preserving modelInstanceId plus authored matrices, and entity deletion unregisters transient state. Invariant 529 covers graph extraction, hierarchy propagation, independent instance time, animated aggregate bounds/entity composition, unchanged persisted matrices, reload/collaboration/lifecycle wiring, and matrix-target rejection. The harness now contains 320 runTest blocks and 2,286 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-17.ordinary-node-animation-runtime-v1 with 653 valid entries. Explicit gaps: Editor clip/playback controls and persisted clip/time/speed state, morph-weight Editor controls/persistence, GPU-compute morph evaluation, KHR_animation_pointer, animation blending/cross-fades, captured Khronos ordinary-node/CUBICSPLINE fixtures, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures remain open. The repository also still lacks an actual Basis Universal decoder/transcoder binary or WASM dependency, worker pool, production KTX2/Basis fixtures, full DFD transfer/alpha interpretation, and decoder lifecycle telemetry. Previous Imported KTX2 Runtime Hook slice: engine/render/materials/TextureManager.js now routes KTX2 byte payloads through the existing GpuTextureLoader instead of createImageBitmap: unsupercompressed containers use native direct upload with their authored mip count and device feature gates, while supercompressed BasisLZ/Zstandard/ZLIB payloads use the existing decode-request contract and an explicitly registered setKtx2Decoder() callback. TextureMath.textureSelectTranscodeTarget() chooses an alpha-compatible, sRGB-aware target from the actual GPU feature set; successful native/decoded resources retain format, dimensions, mip metadata, KTX2 plan, target score, stable cache ownership, and the existing exact glTF sampler path. URL, File, Blob, and direct byte entry points share this route. Strict mode exposes invalid containers or missing decoders; normal material loading returns the caller supplied slot fallback without caching failure. editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js no longer skips compressed image bytes and marks imported KHR_texture_basisu blobs for TextureManager. TextureImport.js remains parser-only and identifies compressed ownership without attempting bitmap decode. Invariant 528 covers native direct upload, cache identity, mip preservation, Basis decode requests, feature-ranked sRGB target selection, decoder registration/removal, strict missing-decoder rejection, KHR_texture_basisu extraction, and imported material routing. The harness now contains 319 runTest blocks and 2,285 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-17.imported-ktx2-runtime-hook-v1 with 653 valid entries. Explicit gaps: the repository still has no actual Basis Universal decoder/transcoder binary or WASM dependency, worker pool, production KTX2/Basis fixtures, full DFD transfer/alpha interpretation, or decoder lifecycle telemetry. Dedicated recursive directory-picker UI, intentional remote HTTP resource policy, exact specular-glossiness shading, clearcoat/transmission/sheen/ior/volume, mip generation for ordinary images, UV sets above 1, sorted alpha-blend/depth-write policy, and alpha-masked shadow-map sampling remain absent. Non-joint node animation channels, morph-weight Editor controls/persistence, GPU-compute morph evaluation, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures also remain open. Previous Loose glTF Package Ownership slice: engine/assets/import/GltfSceneImporter.js now owns bounded local external-resource URI admission and package assembly for loose glTF assets: up to 1,024 selected resources, 4,096-byte URIs, 64 MiB JSON, 512 MiB per resource, and 1 GiB total are admitted; absolute/network URIs, query/fragment ambiguity, backslashes, traversal, duplicate basename ambiguity, missing files, short declared buffers, and excess capacity fail before persistence. editor/js/panels/AssetPanel.js resolves the existing multi-file picker/drop batch through that shared path and writes one self-contained glTF asset with exact buffer and image data URIs, so AssetRegistry, scene reload, undo/redo, export, and collaboration continue using their existing single-asset ownership. Consumed sidecars are not registered as duplicate project assets. editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js now rejects unresolved resources instead of silently fabricating zero-filled buffers and validates embedded/GLB buffer lengths. The full shared importer also resolves external image bytes through its existing cached readExternal callback. Invariant 527 covers nested sibling resolution, exact geometry/texture round trip, sidecar consumption, shared importer image resolution, missing/ambiguous/traversal/network/short-buffer rejection, byte limits, AssetPanel integration, and removal of the zero-buffer fallback. The harness now contains 318 runTest blocks and 2,284 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-17.loose-gltf-package-ownership-v1 with 653 valid entries. Explicit gaps: a dedicated recursive directory-picker UI, editor policy for intentional remote HTTP resources, imported KTX2/Basis transcoder hookup, exact specular-glossiness shading, clearcoat/transmission/sheen/ior/volume, mip generation, UV sets above 1, sorted alpha-blend/depth-write policy, alpha-masked shadow-map sampling, and captured Khronos package/material fixtures remain absent. Non-joint node animation channels, morph-weight Editor controls/persistence, GPU-compute morph evaluation, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures also remain open. Previous glTF Material and Texture Ownership slice: engine/assets/import/GltfSceneImporter.js now exposes one shared recoverable glTF material/texture extraction path, including embedded buffer-view and data-URI images, KHR_texture_basisu source ownership, image MIME data, raw source records, and sampler metadata. editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js runs the existing MaterialImport and TextureImport normalizers, assigns deterministic asset-scoped material IDs to every primitive placement, registers those records through the existing MaterialLibrary and TextureManager, and restores the same resources through SceneManager and history reload callers. Base-color, normal, metallic-roughness, emissive, and occlusion slots retain independent glTF samplers, color-space ownership, TEXCOORD_0/TEXCOORD_1 selection, and KHR_texture_transform offset/scale/rotation. TextureManager.js owns explicit blob cache keys, sRGB GPU formats, and exact cached WebGPU sampler descriptors. EntityMeshRenderer.js extends its existing material bind group and vertex ABI rather than adding a renderer: imported materials now apply packed roughness/metallic channels, normal scale, emissive factor/texture, occlusion strength, alpha mask cutoff, unlit workflow, two UV sets, and per-slot transforms/samplers. Tangent-space vertex splitting and skinned buffer refresh preserve UV1. Viewport.js resolves an explicit entity material override first and otherwise uses each part material; collaboration snapshots validate and preserve the deterministic material ID. Invariant 526 covers normalized factors, five texture slots, embedded bytes, sampler enums, UV transforms, UV1, deterministic IDs, runtime registration, shader ownership, alpha admission, collaboration round trip, and tangent-remap preservation. The harness now contains 317 runTest blocks and 2,283 assert calls. Registry version is 2026-07-17.gltf-material-texture-ownership-v1 with 653 valid entries. Explicit gaps: external sibling files for loose .gltf packages, imported KTX2/Basis transcoder hookup, exact specular-glossiness shading, material extensions such as clearcoat/transmission/sheen/ior/volume, mip generation, UV sets above 1, sorted alpha-blend/depth-write policy, alpha-masked shadow-map sampling, and captured Khronos material fixtures remain absent. Non-joint node animation channels, morph-weight Editor controls/persistence, GPU-compute morph evaluation, exact nonidentity skinned-node fixtures, and captured multi-node/multi-skin fixtures also remain open. Previous Model Node Affine Ownership slice: editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js walks the active glTF scene hierarchy, composes exact column-major node world matrices, expands repeated mesh-node references, preserves node-specific morph defaults, and selects each node skin. Exact per-part affine metadata survives reload, duplication, collaboration, culling, focus, raycast, and rendering. Previous Animated Spatial Bounds slice: engine/core/math/MathGeometry.js now owns a strict transformed-AABB bounding-sphere report that normalizes finite quaternions, rotates scaled off-origin local centers, and computes a conservative radius from all three scaled half-extents. editor/js/EditorApp.js uses that report with the authored or live physics pose, while editor/js/utils/SpatialIndex.js rejects malformed entries, snapshots mutable positions, skips exact duplicate updates, and removes failed out-of-world insertions. engine/render/mesh/EntityMeshRenderer.js advances a monotonic bounds revision only when animated min/max/radius values change, and editor/js/panels/Viewport.js forwards each new revision once in edit mode and refreshes live physics poses through the same mesh-scoped path in play mode, so only entities sharing that mesh reach the existing Editor octree/query broadphase and exact duplicate poses do not reinsert. Invariant 523 covers rotated off-origin centers, nonuniform scale, conservative diagonal radius, quaternion normalization/rejection, immutable and duplicate-skipping octree updates, invalid/out-of-world rejection, renderer revision wiring, live physics rotation ownership, Viewport synchronization, public export, and registry exposure. Explicit gaps: physics-collider deformation from animated visual bounds, GPU-compute morph evaluation, multi-node transform/default-weight and per-primitive material ownership, morph-weight Editor controls/persistence, captured Khronos AnimatedMorphCube fixtures, cursor-ray anchoring, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Morph Runtime slice: engine/core/math/MeshAttributeMath.js owns bounded reusable-buffer morph attribute evaluation and exact morphed position bounds across up to 4,194,304 vertices and 256 targets. editor/js/modules/ModelImporter.js preserves POSITION/NORMAL/TANGENT deltas, conventional target names, target node ownership, and glTF node-weight precedence over mesh defaults. engine/render/mesh/SkeletalAnimation.js samples packed LINEAR/STEP/CUBICSPLINE weight channels, applies deltas before skinning, exposes strict weight control, and retains GPU skinning for zero-weight meshes while active morphs use the existing CPU upload path. Invariant 522 covers import, target metadata, exact deformation, runtime animation/manual weights, pre-skinning order, cached bounds, malformed input rejection, GPU fallback policy, reload ownership, public export, and registry exposure. Previous Skinned Bounds slice: engine/core/math/MeshAttributeMath.js now owns strict on-demand bounds for four-influence skinned positions across up to 4,194,304 vertices and 65,536 joints, rejecting malformed attribute lengths, non-finite or over-range values, out-of-range joint indices, and weights outside the normalized tolerance. engine/render/mesh/SkeletalAnimation.js derives bounds from immutable bind positions plus the live skin matrices, so CPU skinning, GPU skinning with intentionally stale CPU output positions, and ragdoll bone transforms share one authoritative result without GPU readback. engine/tools/camera/CameraController.js gives explicitly supplied runtime geometry precedence over static collider or mesh bounds, while editor/js/EditorApp.js supplies that descriptor only for registered skinned meshes; existing non-skinned crater and dent deformation continues through EntityMeshRenderer mesh registration and cached mesh bounds. Invariant 521 covers exact weighted transforms, world-space nonuniform scaling and translation, runtime-bound precedence, live GPU matrix movement with stale CPU positions, ragdoll matrix ownership, malformed joints/weights/matrices/capacity rejection, Editor integration, public export, and registry ownership. Explicit gaps: runtime morph-target application and bounds, animated broadphase/frustum bound refresh and caching policy, cursor-ray anchoring, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, device profiles, captured skinning/focus fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Compound Collider slice: engine/ecs/EntitySchema.js now preserves the established compoundColliders array through Collider normalization and declares the already-shipped cylinder shape instead of coercing it to box. engine/tools/camera/CameraController.js admits 1..1,024 compound children, strict box/sphere/capsule/cylinder dimensions, bounded offsets, and unit local rotations; nested compounds, unsupported shapes, malformed vectors, and excess capacity fail closed. It transforms every child corner around that child's local offset and rotation before applying entity nonuniform scale, quaternion rotation, and translation, then feeds the exact conservative child union through the existing entity-focus path. Parent placeholder colliders are ignored when valid compound children exist, matching current Viewport and physics ownership. Invariant 520 covers schema enum/field preservation, real Collider registration and archetype round trip, child-local rotation, entity world transforms, exact aggregate bounds, malformed offset/rotation/shape/nesting/capacity rejection, live Editor creation ownership, Viewport consumption, spawnable contracts, and source delegation. Explicit gaps: runtime-deformed/skinned bounds, cursor-ray anchoring, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, device profiles, captured focus fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Entity Bounds slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now owns strict bounded sphere-fit, multi-AABB aggregation, and bounds-focus reports. engine/tools/camera/CameraController.js extracts local box, sphere, capsule, cylinder, mesh, or point bounds; transforms all eight corners by admitted scale, unit quaternion, and world position; aggregates up to 1,024 entities; and routes the resulting center/radius through the existing third-person focus and transition path. editor/js/EditorApp.js now resolves complete ECS Transform/Collider/Renderable records plus cached EntityMeshRenderer bounds for visible selections. Its existing viewport.focus F command frames one or many entities, while Viewport.js exposes validated camera-position/live-position accessors and UpdateStages.js advances the existing Tween with manual-movement cancellation. ToolsImports and EngineBootstrap expose the pure and stateful reports. Invariant 519 covers aggregate center/extents/radius, limiting-FOV distance, nonuniform scale, quaternion rotation, primitive collider and mesh fallback, malformed/reversed/capacity rejection, live focus mutation, Editor ECS/mesh ownership, F-hotkey registration, viewport accessors, tween execution, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: animated Editor look-at orientation, skinned/deformed runtime bounds, compound colliders, cursor-ray anchoring, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, device profiles, captured focus fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Animated Focus slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now owns standardCameraFocusTransitionReport(), a strict absolute-elapsed transition from immutable start target/distance to admitted focus goal. It validates finite bounded vectors, distances, elapsed time, duration, and frame delta; shared MathScalar.lerp() and smoothstep() produce exact endpoints, and completion tolerance makes 30/60/120 Hz runs converge identically. CameraController.js persists active transition state under the existing third-person camera, advances only after valid nonzero viewport updates, replaces interrupted focus from the current admitted pose, and clears transitions on completion, first-person switches, snap requests, or user zoom so input is not overwritten. Direct focus calls retain zero-duration snap compatibility; existing phase-4 and Spell Builder mode switches animate through the new 0.25-second config default. Editor keeps its separate Tween/focus ownership unchanged. EngineState and EngineDefaultConfig expose bounded duration policy, and ToolsImports/EngineBootstrap expose pure and wrapper reports. Invariant 518 covers midpoint easing, exact endpoints, malformed and oversized timing rejection, immutable absolute interpolation, 30/120 Hz equivalence, snap compatibility, persisted live state, zero-size frame retention, zoom/mode cancellation, config loading, live callers, Editor isolation, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: cursor-ray-anchored zoom/focus, automatic collider/mesh bounds extraction, multi-object framing, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, device profiles, captured focus fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Focus Framing slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now owns a strict focus report for finite world targets, retained or explicit distance, and aspect-aware sphere framing. Sphere fit uses the limiting horizontal/vertical half-FOV and paddedRadius / sin(limitingHalfFov), then applies ordered zoom bounds; point-only focus retains authored zoom exactly. CameraController.js exposes admitted focus mutation, resets stale collision resolution, and fixes the existing third-person mode switch so its already-supplied lookAtTarget is no longer ignored. Phase-4 simulation and Spell Builder pass their current config through those established mode-switch calls, while Editor retains its separate persisted F-key and tween-camera ownership. EngineState and EngineDefaultConfig expose focus padding, and ToolsImports/EngineBootstrap expose pure and stateful reports. Invariant 517 covers square and portrait sphere fit, limiting-FOV selection, explicit-distance precedence, retained-distance target changes, bound clamping, malformed targets/radius/aspect/padding, collision-state reset, first-person look targeting, config loading, live callers, Editor ownership, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: cursor-ray-anchored zoom/focus, automatic collider/mesh bounds extraction, multi-object framing, animated standard-camera target transitions, volumetric camera sweeps/filtering, shoulder probes, device profiles, captured focus fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Collision Boom slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now owns a strict collision-boom report that validates target/desired-eye geometry, delegates hit admission to shared SceneQueryMath.sceneQueryHitReport(), applies bounded target offset and surface clearance, retracts immediately on obstruction, and restores distance through exact elapsed exponential response. CameraController.js keeps authored camera.tp.distance separate from collision-resolved camera.tp.resolvedDistance, invokes an optional backend query only for third-person frames, contains query failures, and uses the resolved eye for the view matrix, ECS camera sync, and RayPicking origin. EngineState and EngineDefaultConfig expose enabled/clearance/target-offset/minimum/recovery policy; phase-4 simulation and Spell Builder bind the callback to their existing PhysX raycastWorld without modifying physics ownership; ToolsImports and EngineBootstrap expose the reports. Invariant 516 covers query geometry, shared hit validation, immediate clearance retraction, malformed hit rejection, desired-distance preservation, exact 30/120 Hz recovery, resolved basis/picking, first-person isolation, config, live callers, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: volumetric sphere/capsule camera sweeps, collision-layer and target-entity filtering, shoulder/near-plane probes, zoom-to-cursor/focus targets, device profiles, persisted controls UI, captured camera-obstruction fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Zoom slice: engine/tools/input/StandardInputController.js now captures finite wheel deltas on the standard 3D canvas, accumulates them under a hard bound, exposes one-shot consumption, and derives a continuous zoom axis from admitted left/right trigger values. StandardCameraController.js owns a strict third-person zoom report with ordered positive distance limits, bounded wheel sensitivity/gamepad rate, exponent overflow protection, multiplicative wheel scaling, and elapsed trigger scaling via distance * exp(axis * rate * dt); valid 30, 60, and 120 Hz trigger updates therefore reach the same distance. First-person behavior remains unchanged, third-person distance is clamped before eye construction, and CameraController consumes wheel input only after a successful update. EngineState, EngineDefaultConfig, phase-4 simulation, Spell Builder, ToolsImports, and EngineBootstrap propagate zoom state/config/reports through current ownership. Invariant 515 covers accumulation/saturation, malformed deltas, wheel capture/consumption, trigger derivation, analytic multiplicative scaling, extreme exponent/distance clamps, ordered-bound rejection, 30/120 Hz equivalence, first/live ownership, config, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: collision-aware boom/sweeps and zoom-to-cursor/focus targets, device profiles, haptic cooldown/rate policy, persisted shared controls UI, captured input fixtures, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Advanced Gamepad slice: engine/tools/input/StandardInputController.js now extends the existing standard-gamepad poll with strict button/trigger reports, bounded button indices and thresholds, radially shaped right-stick look with configurable axes/deadzone/gamma/inversion, absent-stick zero fallback, standard mapping diagnostics, A/B/L3 action tokens, disconnect cleanup, haptics capability/effect admission, and fail-contained dual-rumble execution. StandardCameraController.js converts admitted look axes into elapsed angular motion so 30, 60, and 120 Hz produce the same turn; mouse and gamepad deltas compose before existing pitch clamps in both camera modes. CameraController, EngineState, EngineDefaultConfig, phase-4 simulation, Spell Builder, and EngineBootstrap propagate look axes, trigger state, optional radians-per-second configuration, and public reports through current ownership. Invariant 514 covers right-stick shaping/inversion availability, missing-axis fallback, malformed axes/buttons, trigger values, button action tokens, disconnect reset, haptic bounds/capability/execution, 30/120 Hz turn equivalence, camera limits, live callers, config, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: device-specific profiles/remapping, haptic cooldown/rate/visibility policy, persisted cross-subsystem action profiles, shared settings/rebinding UI, modifier chords/sequences, accessibility presets, captured keyboard-layout/gamepad/haptic fixtures, nonlinear response profiles, collision/zoom, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Action Map slice: engine/tools/input/StandardInputController.js now owns a bounded 16-action standard 3D input map with physical KeyboardEvent.code bindings, optional key:<character> tokens, per-action binding limits, duplicate/unknown/missing-action rejection, cross-action conflict reporting, atomic runtime rebinding, defensive reads, text-input gating, held-state reset on blur/detach, and translation into the established camera key shape. Default WASD, Space/Control, Shift sprint, camera modes, Delete, arrows, and character-specific +/- behavior remain compatible; shifted physical WASD no longer changes identity. Existing Editor HotkeyManager persistence/UI, WebGPU OS scoped shortcut registry, and First Shard CrownInput action bindings remain their subsystem owners rather than being replaced. Invariant 513 covers complete-map admission, duplicates, conflicts, physical/character resolution, atomic rejection/success, shifted input, text gating, blur reset, defensive copies, live listener teardown, sibling-system ownership, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: persisted cross-subsystem action-profile import/export, shared settings/rebinding UI, modifier chords/sequences, accessibility presets, trigger/right-stick/haptics policy, captured keyboard-layout/gamepad fixtures, nonlinear response profiles, collision/zoom, roll, and stereo/WebXR remain absent. Previous Camera Response slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now owns a bounded first-order movement-response report that applies separate acceleration and deceleration time constants through shared MathScalar.damp(). It persists one admitted local response-axis vector, computes the exact exponential endpoint plus exact frame-average integral, and uses that average for displacement so both response state and traveled distance agree across 30, 60, and 120 Hz. Zero-second defaults preserve the established instant keyboard/gamepad behavior and exact one-second distances; malformed state/times reject before mutation, zero delta is stable, third-person updates and explicit mode switches clear latent first-person momentum, and zero-size frames retain prior state. CameraController.js, EngineState.js, EngineDefaultConfig.js, ToolsImports, and EngineBootstrap propagate the optional profile through existing ownership. Invariant 512 covers analytic endpoint/average values, acceleration and release equivalence across update rates, instant-default compatibility, malformed bounds, state/config/wrapper propagation, mode reset, shared damp delegation, and public exposure. Explicit gaps: nonlinear response curves and speed-dependent profiles, configurable action maps and rebinding UI, trigger/right-stick/haptics policy, collision/boom sweeps, roll, zoom policy, reversed-Z/infinite-far projection, stereo/WebXR, and captured camera/gamepad fixtures remain absent. Previous Analog Camera/Input slice: engine/core/math/InputSignalMath.js now owns strict signed-axis remapping and multi-axis radial deadzone reports with bounded inner/outer deadzones, response gamma, inversion, proportional magnitude, and unit-radius normalization. engine/tools/input/StandardInputController.js polls the browser gamepad list, maps standard left-stick X/Y into the camera local right/forward convention, clears disconnected state, and exposes shaped movement axes without moving browser capture into MathEngine. StandardCameraController.js composes those proportional analog axes with admitted keyboard axes, saturates each local component, and applies one shared radial unit-speed cap before world-space displacement; keyboard-only cardinal, cancellation, diagonal, and elapsed-time behavior remains unchanged. CameraController.js, engine input state, phase-4 simulation, and Spell Builder propagate the live axes, while the public bootstrap and registry expose the new contracts. Invariant 511 covers deadzone boundaries, inner-to-outer remapping, gamma/inversion admission, diagonal normalization, malformed axes, standard gamepad signs, disconnect reset, analog proportional speed, keyboard-plus-stick cancellation/saturation, live callers, registry entries, and public exports. Explicit gaps: acceleration/deceleration and smoothing, configurable action maps and rebinding UI, trigger/right-stick camera policies, haptics, collision/boom sweeps, roll, zoom policy, reversed-Z/infinite-far projection, stereo/WebXR, and captured runtime gamepad fixtures remain absent. Previous Normalized Camera Movement slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js now combines first-person W/S, A/D, and vertical Space/Control input into one admitted world-space movement intent before displacement. Recognized movement keys must be boolean, opposite keys cancel exactly, cardinal direction and elapsed distance remain unchanged, and two- or three-axis input is normalized through shared vector primitives so diagonal movement cannot exceed the selected walk/sprint distance. The report exposes local axes, active-axis count, raw magnitude, normalization status, direction, delta, and applied distance; updates also reject a finite move that would escape the bounded world-coordinate domain. The limits and reports are exported through the actual public bootstrap. Invariant 510 covers cardinal motion, square-root-two and square-root-three diagonals, all-axis cancellation, malformed keys/yaw, world overflow, public delegation, and equal one-second diagonal distance at 30 and 120 Hz. Explicit gaps: analog input/deadzone/remapping, acceleration/deceleration and smoothing, collision/boom sweeps, roll, zoom policy, reversed-Z/infinite-far projection, stereo/WebXR, and captured runtime fixtures remain absent. Previous Elapsed Camera Movement slice: standard camera movement now uses bounded elapsed-time math across engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js, CameraController.js, engine/core/AppBootstrap.js, tests/phase4_simulation/main.js, and tests/spell_builder/src/main.js. The existing 0.1 walk and 0.3 sprint values remain reference steps at 60 Hz, omitted delta remains exactly one 60 Hz step for compatibility, and admitted 0..0.1 second deltas scale distance by deltaSeconds * 60. The fixed-step app loop now passes its canonical simulation delta into phase-4 camera updates, while Spell Builder passes its already bounded RAF delta, producing equal one-second walk/sprint distance at 30, 60, and 120 Hz without introducing another clock. Invalid, negative, non-finite, or oversized deltas reject before camera mutation; the wrapper records the accepted delta. Invariant 509 covers rate equivalence, legacy no-delta behavior, sprint distance, delta bounds, wrapper propagation, and both live caller paths. Explicit gaps: simultaneous-axis input still moves faster diagonally, and camera collision/boom sweeps, smoothing/acceleration, roll, zoom policy, reversed-Z/infinite-far projection, stereo/WebXR, and captured runtime fixtures remain absent. Previous Standard Camera slice: engine/tools/camera/StandardCameraController.js and CameraController.js now own a bounded standard-camera math boundary without replacing the engine camera. Shared reports admit numeric 1/2 camera modes, finite first/third-person positions, target, yaw/pitch, distance, mouse deltas, log clocks, 0..10,000,000 viewport dimensions, ordered positive near/far planes, 1..179 degree vertical FOV, sensitivity, and walk/sprint steps before matrix or state mutation. Valid behavior retains the 45-degree FOV, 0.1 near plane, 100 far fallback, first-person movement, pitch limits, third-person orbit, and WebGPU projection. Camera bases accept both flat public state and the live nested engine state, use shared vector/clamp math, and reject malformed state instead of hiding it behind Number(...) || default. The stateful wrapper retains prior matrices on invalid or zero-size frames, consumes input only after a successful update, preserves third-person targets, publishes pickFovRadians for RayPicking.js, bounds ECS synchronization, and validates mode/look-target switches without assuming a browser document. The actual EngineBootstrap.js entry exports all limits and reports. Remaining aggregation classification: The compatible bounded/reporting mean pass is complete. Retained by design: MathSignal.signalMean() performs per-sample finite validation; syncPlanner.averagePhase() is a circular mean; AnimalLocomotion support points and WheelSpinVisual are vector centroids; MathQuality, SignalQualityMath, and rendering/audio blends are weighted aggregates; ProxyGeometrySystem uses a zero-allocation per-frame typed-array prefix loop; TransitionEngine, ComputedStyle, and telemetry use cumulative running totals; and ProceduralWeather.getAverageStats() combines three scalar means with a predicate-derived sunshine proportion. These require category-specific helpers or would add allocations/alter validation semantics, so they are tracked as retained rather than falsely marked missing.

Previous gameplay slice: engine/gameplay/spells/SpellGenerator.js preserves deterministic spell ID and particle-effect seed/element-char hash vectors while delegating raw weighted seed/char XOR arithmetic to MathBits.js. Previous voxel slice: engine/voxel/TextureArrayManager.js preserves material texture noise hash/noise vectors while delegating prime-coordinate XOR hash arithmetic to MathBits.js. Recent voxel slice: engine/voxel/ChunkRegistry.js preserves content xxHash, voxel sampling policy, and Bloom filter coordinate hashes while delegating byte/hash arithmetic to shared ChecksumMath.js. Gate 1 Pass Contract Locked

MD/engine/math-contract.md defines matrix layout, multiplication order, quaternion order, WebGPU depth, units, tolerances, deterministic rules, worker-safety, and WGSL parity expectations.

Ready In-Place Foundation

The current math stack is broad enough to become the seed of MathEngine: vectors, matrices, quats, geometry, packing, noise, color, grids, BVH, spatial hash, physics helpers, and shader chunks already exist.

Upgrade Audit Standard

A proper upgrade audit must prove the target architecture is justified, identify blockers, map current coverage to desired capability, define module boundaries, rank gaps by dependency order, and state test gates for accepting each phase. Findings still need severity, evidence, root-cause notes, remediation, and closure criteria.

Upgrade Audit Requirement Applied Here
Target architecture stated Yes MathEngine is defined as a pure JavaScript shared value engine.
Current-vs-target gap matrix Yes Existing modules are mapped against the proposed MathEngine categories.
Upgrade blockers identified Yes P0 math safety blockers and the math contract were fixed on 2026-06-15. Bootstrap And Consumer Audit

This upgrade must preserve current engine wiring. The correct path is to add or harden math modules under the existing engine, then expose stable APIs through current barrels. Do not replace the engine, rename the public import layer, or force consumers to migrate all at once.

Integration Point What Exists Now Upgrade Rule
engine/core/math/index.js Main math barrel. Exports EngineMath.js, constants, scalar, vec2/3/4, quat, matrix, geometry, packing, random, physics, signal, oscillator, BVH, Bezier, spatial hash, MathBits legacy structure hashes/seeds, prime-XOR coordinate hashes/seeds, signed prime-XOR spatial hash buckets, prime-axis wind turbulence hashes, and union-find modules. All new stable MathEngine modules should be exported here first so EngineImports.Math receives them automatically.
engine/EngineImports.js Imports * as Math from ./core/math/index.js and exports it as the grouped engine math namespace. Keep this stable. New modules appear through EngineImports.Math when exported from core/math/index.js.
engine/MathImports.js Imports EngineImports.Math, exports the grouped Math namespace, and destructures a curated flat list of common functions. Do not dump every future MathEngine function into flat exports. Add only high-use stable APIs; everything else stays available through Math.
engine/EngineBootstrap.js Re-exports flat math from MathImports.js, exports the full Math namespace, and spreads MathAll into the bundled Engine object. Keep compatibility. New math reaches Engine.Math through the namespace; only selected APIs should become top-level Engine.foo().
tests/common/EngineTestImports.js Uses EngineImports.Math but re-exports an old flat subset including OpenGL-style mat4PerspectiveRad. Update tests to prefer WebGPU helpers where rendering is WebGPU. Add invariant tests through the namespace so new modules are covered without expanding the flat subset unnecessarily.
Direct math consumers Many systems import direct files: EngineMath.js, MathQuat.js, MathMat.js, MathGeometry.js, MathRandom.js, MathBits.js, BVHAccel.js. Do not move or break existing files. Add canonical implementations and re-export/delegate from current files during migration.
WebGPU OS consumers Apps and shell code import engine math directly, including pinball camera math, immersive compositor math, GPU UI compositor math, and LLM sync planning bits/oscillator math. Keep engine/core/math/index.js and existing direct module paths stable. New shared math should reduce app-local duplicate helpers over time.
Camera and render math CameraSystem.js uses CameraMath.computeViewProjMatrix(). ThirdPersonCameraSystem.js uses MathImports.js. Some render tests still import EngineMath.js directly. Harden camera/projection tests before broad upgrade. Rendering code should use WebGPU projection helpers unless it explicitly tests OpenGL depth.
GPU layout helpers WGSLStructLayout.js, WGSLStructSize.js, and Std140Layout.js already implement alignment, struct size, field offsets, accessors, and buffer validation. MemoryLayoutMath.js now wraps these helpers under MathEngine. Continue migrating only proven duplicate layout math; do not rewrite working GPU layout code.
Compression helpers engine/core/compression already has VLInt, quantization, octahedral normals, quaternion compression, RLE, delta, palette, LZ77, Huffman, mesh/voxel/animation compression. CompressionMath.js now wraps reusable compression primitive reports over existing compression helpers: RLE, delta, palette, varint/zigzag, entropy lower-bound planning, LZ77 window budgeting, dictionary repeated-sequence analysis, Huffman code-length budgets, range-model probability budgets, and primitive candidate selection. Full mesh/voxel/animation compression remains in compression systems.
Package, save, and storage helpers Package code already has gzip/gunzip, SHA-256, Merkle roots, CRC-32 in deprecated zip, binary container headers, DataView usage, chunk hashes, and cache keys. Core save is a system, not math. Do not move package/security code into MathEngine. Extract only reusable checksum, buffer cursor, format, and byte conversion helpers after tests.

Main Folder Math Usage Map

Focused scan scope: engine/, webgpu-os/, editor/, plauna/, and agi/. Excluded vendored code, PhysX bindings, Kaolin, and output/mirror folders. Source/runtime files scanned: 2,306.

engine/ 1,222 Canonical math modules, camera/projection math, render/shader math, geometry/SDF/BVH/spatial math, world/voxel/storage compression, particles, AI steering, audio/signal, GPU layout, and physics helpers. Upgrade Blockers

Fixed

B-001: mat4Decompose() zero-scale fallback fixed

A transform matrix with any zero scale axis now preserves translation and scale while returning a finite identity-rotation fallback.

Location

engine/core/math/MathMat.js, mat4Decompose()

Evidence

AnimationTimeMath.js is now included in the direct browser evidence for active-duration, fill/direction progress, loop/frame wrapping, playback-rate reports, time-scale/offset sample-time reports, timeline offset preservation, OpenUSD timeCode/layer-offset/time-sample reports, keyframe brackets, glTF sampler interpolation, clip sample-time reports, and keyframe reduction reports.

Root Cause

The function computes sx, sy, and sz, then divides matrix columns by those values without guarding zero or near-zero scale components.

Impact

Closed for collapsed-scale finite output. Broader Gate 0 coverage now also passes inverse, look-at, projection, quaternion round-trip, negative-scale, and camera finite-output checks.

Fix

  • Done Guard abs(scale) < EPSILON before normalization.
  • Done Return identity rotation when rotation cannot be recovered.
  • Add a boolean/status field only if callers need to distinguish fallback from real rotation.

Retest

  • Done Zero scale on X, Y, Z individually.
  • Done All-zero scale.
  • Next Negative scale with nonzero axes.
  • Done Standard TRS round-trip stays unchanged.
{
  "name": "mat4 decompose finite zero scale",
  "pass": true,
  "detail": {
    "translation": [1, 2, 3],
    "rotation": [0, 0, 0, 1],
    "scale": [0, 2, 3]
  }
}

Upgrade Risks

Fixed

U-001: WebGPU rendering test now uses WebGPU projection depth

tests/phase3_rendering/main.js now calls mat4PerspectiveRadWebGPU() for the WebGPU depth pass.

Evidence

tests/math-invariants.html verifies mat4PerspectiveRadWebGPU() and CameraMath.computePerspectiveProjection() map near depth to 0 and far depth to 1.

Impact

Closed for the phase 3 rendering smoke. Browser verification initialized the WebGPU page successfully with no warnings or errors after the helper swap.

Fix / Retest

Done Switched the test to mat4PerspectiveRadWebGPU() and verified the page initializes with WebGPU.

Low

U-004: Duplicate quaternion and matrix surfaces can drift

EngineMath.js, MathQuat.js, and MathMat.js expose overlapping operations.

Evidence

Quaternion multiply/normalize/inverse/rotate and matrix composition exist in multiple modules or compatibility layers.

Impact

Formula drift is possible if one copy is fixed and another remains stale.

Fix / Retest

Pick canonical implementations, make compatibility exports delegate to them, and test both import paths.

Upgrade Readiness Checks

Check Status Observed Result
mat4Inverse() Pass tests/math-invariants.html verifies M * inverse(M) and inverse(M) * M are identity within tolerance.
TRS decompose with nonzero scale Pass Translation, scale, and rotation recover for nonzero TRS; zero-scale fallback and negative nonzero X scale also pass.
Quaternion matrix round-trip Pass quatFromAxisAngle() to mat4FromQuat() to quatFromRotationMatrix() preserves orientation within tolerance.
mat4LookAt() simple case Pass Eye [0,0,5] maps to origin and target [0,0,0] maps to negative Z.
WebGPU projection depth Pass Near maps to 0, far maps to approximately 1 in the invariant harness.
CameraMath.computeViewProjMatrix() Pass View, projection, and view-projection matrices are finite for a basic camera transform in the invariant harness.

Category Coverage Matrix

This is the working checklist for the upgrade: what we already have, where it lives now, what is missing, and what the upgraded MathEngine should expose for each category.

Math Category Status Where It Is Now What We Have What We Need / Should Have
Constants Have engine/core/math/MathConstants.js PI, TAU, EPSILON, conversion constants, common physical constants. Keep as canonical constants/MathConstants.js; add manifest metadata and tolerance policy links.
Number / scalar math Partial MathScalar.js, MathBits.js Clamp, lerp, remap, smoothstep, angle helpers, modulo helpers, power-of-two helpers. MathScalar.js now covers safeDiv, nearlyEqual, roundTo/floorTo/ceilTo, gcd/lcm, finite-number fallbacks, and first-pass explicit range operations. Remaining: caller migration and possible later split to NumberMath.js/RangeMath.js only if the module boundary becomes useful.
Range math Partial MathScalar.js General scalar range helpers now cover normalization, containment, overlap, intersection, union, clamping, and splitting with browser invariant coverage. Still migrate duplicate/private range helpers where behavior can be proven, and keep quantized storage range math in QuantMath.js.
Curve / easing math Have MathEasing.js, BezierCurves.js, MathVec2.js, MathVec3.js MathEasing.js now includes CSS Level 1 easing helpers: cssEasingParseReport(), cssEasingReport(), cssEasingValue(), cssCubicBezierValue(), and cssStepEasing(), plus existing easing functions, Bezier curves, splines, Catmull-Rom, Hermite, and vector interpolation. Plauna TransitionEngine.js is a production caller for exact linear, quadratic, and cubic easing functions and combines them with shared monotonic timing/progress bounds while retaining UI mutation and scheduling policy. CollabCameraInterp.js retains its domain-specific cubic Hermite position/velocity interpolation while moving segment duration, elapsed time, and progress bounds onto shared FrameMath and MathScalar contracts. CollabTransformSync.js likewise retains domain-specific vector lerp and shortest-path quaternion slerp while sharing finite segment/elapsed/extrapolation timing and progress bounds. CollabEntityAuthority.js retains its 100 ms linear ownership-handoff contract through shared monotonic timing and scalar bounds, but direct tracing found no maintained getHandoffBlend() consumer. CollabZoneHandoff.js declares a separate 200 ms blend constant with no runtime read or maintained consumer, so zone blending remains missing rather than marked implemented. Online-Informed Gap Additions

These are additional categories and hardening targets from current official specs, engine docs, and established math library docs. They should be added to the upgrade backlog without turning MathEngine into a new engine.

New / Expanded Category Status In Our Stack Priority Why Add It Concrete Additions Reference
Robust numerics / floating-point policy Partial High engine/core/math/RobustNumericMath.js now covers float64 classification for NaN, infinity, signed zero, normal/subnormal values, ULP spacing/distance, nextafter stepping, Neumaier compensated sum, pairwise sum, stable-sum condition/cancellation reports, and filtered 2D orientation predicate reports. MathRegistry.js exposes math.robust-numeric, and tests/math-invariants.html checks the edge cases in browser. Still needed: caller migration for existing private numerically-stable formulas, project-wide finite/NaN policy adoption, exact/adaptive predicate expansion for geometry decisions that cannot tolerate filtered uncertainty, and CPU/WGSL parity policy for f32-vs-f64 tolerance tiers. IEEE 754-2019, Goldberg floating-point paper, Shewchuk robust predicates
Affine / basis / shear transform math Partial High We have TRS matrices and quaternions, but the audit does not clearly separate rigid, affine, basis, scale, and shear semantics. This is important for editor transforms, importers, animation, and decomposition. RigidTransformMath.js, AffineTransformMath.js, BasisMath.js, orthonormalize, extractBasis, composeBasis, shear detection, determinant sign, handedness checks. Unity Mathematics, Godot Basis, Godot Transform3D
Projection variants and clip-space contracts Partial High We already caught OpenGL/WebGPU depth differences. The missing part is an explicit projection family and tests for finite, infinite, orthographic, reversed-Z, jittered, and oblique projections. ProjectionMath.js, perspective/ortho NO and ZO variants, infinite far, reversed-Z, jittered projection, oblique clip plane, unproject test vectors. glMatrix Mat4 projection docs, WebGPU spec

Target MathEngine Architecture

The attached plan upgrades the target from a 3D/game math library into a shared pure-JavaScript value engine. The rule is clear: MathEngine owns reusable value transformation, conversion, packing, measurement, validation, hashing, scoring, and estimation. It must not own rendering, networking, media playback, codecs, gameplay, app behavior, or renderer-specific effects.

Target Category Current Coverage Upgrade Required
Number / scalar math Strong MathScalar.js, MathConstants.js, UnitMath.js, and vector helpers cover clamp, lerp, smoothstep, wrapping, constants, safe-number helpers, deterministic step rounding, gcd/lcm, first-pass range operations, core scalar unit conversions, and the first migrated engine angle call sites. Next: migrate duplicate private helpers where proven, extend MathPrecision.js only where tolerance policy needs more surface, and split NumberMath/RangeMath later only if the boundary earns its keep.
Vector / matrix / quaternion math Strong Existing MathVec2/3/4, MathMat, MathQuat, and MathDualQuat. Zero-scale decomposition fixed. Add broader invariant tests and canonicalize duplicate legacy exports.
Conversion math Partial Color conversion exists, UnitMath.js covers core scalar unit/time/audio/storage conversions, first engine and WebGPU OS Paint/Pinball/Calculator angle caller batches now use it, and TextureMath.js covers texture UV/pixel conversions. Broader coordinate, buffer, material, and renderer-specific conversion policy is still scattered. Add only proven CoordinateConvert, BufferConvert, and MaterialConvert helpers as callers are migrated. Continue caller migration without duplicating UnitMath.js, TextureMath.js, or WorldTime.js.
Bit / buffer / layout math Partial MathBits.js, BufferMath.js, MathPacking.js, MemoryLayoutMath.js, and GPU layout utilities under engine/core/gpu. BufferMath.js now covers endian-safe DataView reads/writes, UTF-8 helpers, bounds reports, byte concatenation, and cursors. MemoryLayoutMath.js covers reusable alignment, WGSL/std140, interleaved, planar, and AoS/SoA offset reports. Remaining: caller migrations and deeper bit packing helpers.
Quantization / packing Partial Float16, SNORM/UNORM, variable bit-depth range packers, raw Uint8x4, vec2 lanes, TRS10 transform scalar packing, editor timeline compression migrated, editor undo-history compression migrated, particle position/velocity state packing, worker signed-range SNORM16 snapshot quantization, ParticleSnapshotDelta.js tiered bit-depth quantization, material ORM/PBR params, voxel material uniforms, oct normals, smallest-three quats, and packed color helpers exist with round-trip/error-bound tests. Domain compression reports now cover mesh, hybrid mesh, voxel chunks, hybrid voxels, vector animation tracks, and quaternion animation tracks. Asset-pipeline accessor math now covers glTF normalized quantized accessor reads, morph target quantized attribute decode, thumbnail-preview GLB accessor/index reads, meshopt metadata, scale/bias metadata, and index bounds. Incremental Upgrade Control Plan

Use this plan for every math upgrade. The goal is steady replacement of scattered or weak math with tested shared helpers, without replacing the engine, breaking imports, or moving system-owned behavior into core math.

Step Required Work Inventory Marking Rule Breakage Guard
1. Pick one row Choose exactly one category from the Simple Upgrade Board or Category Coverage Matrix. Set the row action to In progress only when files are being changed. No broad category rewrites. Do not combine unrelated math systems in one pass.
2. Audit current users Search the five maintained roots: engine, webgpu-os, editor, plauna, agi. Add or update the Where It Is Now cell with confirmed file paths. Do not move files or rename public exports during audit-only work.
3. Add focused tests Add known-vector, edge-case, invariant, and round-trip tests before or with the implementation. Leave Have? as Partial until tests cover the upgraded behavior. Tests must include degenerate input, non-finite input where relevant, and existing consumer behavior.
4. Implement in place Upgrade existing modules first. Add new modules only when the category has no correct home. Update the row action with the exact module created or modified. Keep current import paths stable. Re-export or delegate from legacy files when needed.
5. Wire barrels carefully Export stable APIs through engine/core/math/index.js. Add flat MathImports.js exports only for high-use APIs. Mark Registry / Manifest work only when metadata exists, not just exports. Do not dump every helper into top-level Engine.
6. Run compatibility checks Run the smallest relevant browser/ESM tests plus the affected bundle dry run when practical. Do not mark Yes until verification is recorded in the row or section notes. If a consumer breaks, revert only the new change and keep the audit notes.
7. Mark completion Update status, priority, action, and evidence in this HTML after the code passes checks. No means absent. Partial means exists but untested, scattered, or incomplete. Yes means implemented, exported, tested, and used safely. No category is complete without tests and at least one confirmed consumer path.
Work Batch Size Allowed Scope Stop Immediately If
Bug fix One function and its tests. Example: mat4Decompose() zero-scale handling. A public behavior changes without a compatibility path.
Small helper module One new pure module, one barrel export, one focused test file/page. The helper starts depending on DOM, renderer state, gameplay state, Node, TypeScript, or a build step.
Consumer migration One consumer area at a time: render, editor, OS app, AGI, storage, or shader path. The migration requires changing unrelated systems in the same pass.
WGSL parity One CPU helper plus one WGSL mirror and known-vector parity checks. CPU and shader results disagree outside the documented tolerance.

Upgrade Build Phases

Phase Modules Unlocks
Phase 0: Audit hardening mat4Decompose() zero-scale fallback fixed, Gate 0 invariants pass, and MD/engine/math-contract.md exists. Stops existing transform corruption and establishes the rules future modules must obey.
Phase 1: Core foundation NumberMath, RangeMath, UnitMath, ConversionMath, BitMath, BufferMath, MemoryLayoutMath, FormatMath, PackingMath, QuantMath, ChecksumMath, ValidationMath. Binary files, GPU buffers, packets, saves, packages, deterministic shared helpers.
Phase 2: Entropy and compression EntropyMath, CompressionMath, LossyCompressionMath, FrequencyMath, HistogramMath, ProbabilityMath, lossless/lossy compression math, QualityMetricMath. Compression primitive reports, entropy lower bounds, compression scoring, asset analysis, packet analysis, media prep, randomness health, package optimization.
Phase 3: Visual and material math ColorMath, BlendMath, TextureMath, MaterialMath, ShaderMath, WGSLRegistry. Shader reuse, material reuse, texture tools, UI themes, distance texture logic, packed PBR channels.
Phase 4: Signal, media, and input ImageMath, MathSignal, SamplingMath, FilterMath, InputSignalMath, AudioMath, AudioAutomationMath, FrameMath, and VideoMath are started. ImageData/RGBA helpers, first-pass signal measurement/resampling helpers, sample-grid/window/rate reports, reusable kernel/signal/image filter helpers, dilate/erode morphology, temporal smoothing/noise reduction, pointer pressure/tilt/velocity/smoothing/prediction/stabilization helpers, pure PCM/audio-array helpers, AudioParam automation/envelope/biquad coefficient/response helpers, frame difference/motion/residual/scene-change/timing reports, and sequence-level video reports are covered; next are caller migrations, real pointer/video/canvas/audio automation fixtures, thumbnail rendering, waveform previews, spectrogram fixtures, channel-layout compatibility, large-buffer/perf fixtures, and future codec foundations.
Phase 5: Geometry and layout GridMath, RectMath, ShapeMath, PolygonMath, SDFMath, GraphMath, TopologyMath. Editor tools, panel layout, sprite atlases, voxel grids, node graphs, material graphs, package dependency graphs.
Phase 6: Network and radio NetworkMetricMath, JitterMath, PacketMath, QueueMath, CongestionMath, MeshMath, SignalQualityMath, ChannelMath, and RadioMath are started; caller migrations and captured telemetry fixtures remain open. P2P scoring, WebRTC diagnostics, mesh health, packet budgeting, wireless diagnostics, stream quality estimation.
Phase 7: Registry and tools MathRegistry, MathManifest, auto docs, generated tests, benchmark viewer, entropy viewer, color/curve/compression/WGSL previews. Inspectable OS-level MathEngine with search, docs, previews, shader chunk injection, package metadata, and tooling support.

Upgrade Boundary Rules

Belongs In MathEngine

  • Transforms, converts, packs, unpacks, samples, validates, scores, hashes, estimates, aligns, or measures values.
  • Reusable formulas used by many systems.
  • Pure JavaScript CPU functions and matching WGSL string helpers.
  • Worker-safe functions with no DOM dependency.

Stays Outside MathEngine

  • Existing render modules, shader effect modules, physics systems, simulation systems, AI systems, network drivers, radio/diagnostic bridges, package systems, save systems, replay systems, AppFactory, editor tools, and OS apps.
  • Full codecs, full archive writers, sockets, WebRTC transports, media playback, renderer-specific effects, gameplay, app behavior.
  • Specific fire/water/portal/spell/terrain/theme shaders. Those use ShaderMath but stay in shader effect or render modules.

Gate Required Evidence Failure Means
Gate 0: Existing math safe Pass tests/math-invariants.html passes 34 browser checks and phase 3 rendering smoke initializes with WebGPU projection depth. Closed. Continue to CPU/WGSL parity and Phase 1 foundation tests before expanding MathEngine modules.
Gate 1: Contract locked Pass MD/engine/math-contract.md defines matrix layout, multiplication order, handedness, forward axis, clip-space depth, angle units, tolerance policy, deterministic rules, worker-safety rules, and WGSL mirror rules. Closed. Continue to CPU/WGSL parity and Phase 1 foundation tests.
Gate 2: Phase 1 foundation testable Number, range, unit, conversion, bit, buffer, layout, packing, quantization, checksum, and validation modules have edge-case tests, deterministic tests, and known-vector tests where applicable. Do not build entropy/compression/media/network modules on top of unstable primitives.
Gate 3: Registry metadata complete Every stable function has id, category, version, determinism flag, lossy/lossless flag, shader-safe flag, worker-safe flag, input/output metadata, tags, CPU implementation, and WGSL mirror when applicable. Do not expose MathEngine broadly to apps, packages, editor tools, or shader assembly.

tests/wgsl-chunk-compile.html passes 131 reusable WGSL module compile checks. Do not treat JavaScript as source of truth for all shader behavior yet. Expand known-vector parity for remaining shader-safe WGSL chunk functions before closing Gate 4.
Gate 5: Worker/runtime compatibility Core modules run in main thread, worker context, and browser ES module context without DOM, Node, native, TypeScript, or build-step requirements. MathEngine is not a shared OS-level foundation yet.
Gate 6: Downstream adoption proven At least one render/shader path, one package/save path, one editor/tool path, and one simulation or physics path use MathEngine APIs without private duplicate math. The upgrade is only a library addition, not an ecosystem foundation.

Upgrade Audit References

Technical Sources Checked For Gap Additions

  • IEEE 754-2019: floating-point formats, operations, interchange, and exception conditions.
  • Goldberg floating-point paper: practical floating-point accuracy and rounding-error behavior.
  • Shewchuk robust predicates: adaptive precision predicates for robust geometry decisions.
  • W3C WGSL specification: shader numeric types, vectors, matrices, memory views, texture/sampler functions, and data packing.
  • W3C WebGPU specification: WebGPU rendering, texture, color-space, and GPU API contracts.
  • Khronos glTF 2.0 specification: transform animation, morph weights, accessors, skins, and asset math constraints.
  • Khronos KTX 2.0 specification: GPU texture container, mip, level, layer, face, and supercompression structure.
  • Unity Mathematics package: reference surface for vectors, matrices, quaternions, rigid transforms, random, noise, and affine transforms.
  • Godot Basis and Transform3D: separate basis, transform, scale, shear, and origin concepts.
  • glMatrix Mat4 docs: projection families, WebGPU depth range, transform composition, and matrix utility coverage.
  • W3C WebCodecs specification: browser audio/video/image frame interfaces and media timing surfaces.
  • W3C Web Audio API: audio graph, processing, synthesis, and automation context for reusable DSP math.
  • W3C WebXR Device API and WebXR Depth Sensing: pose, view, projection, sensor-space, and depth-map math surfaces.
  • W3C Pointer Events: pointer pressure, pen geometry, coalesced samples, and predicted input surfaces.
  • Unicode UAX #29: grapheme, word, and sentence boundary rules relevant to text metric/layout math.
  • Unicode UAX #9: Unicode bidirectional algorithm reference used to scope first-strong bidi run audit coverage.
  • Unicode UAX #14: Unicode line-breaking classes and opportunities used to scope deterministic line-break audit coverage.
  • OpenColorIO: color-management reference for display/working-space pipeline categories.
  • W3C Compositing and Blending Level 1: source-over compositing, Porter-Duff operators, and separable blend-mode formulas.
  • W3C Web Animations and CSS Easing: timing, keyframe, iteration, and easing models.
  • OpenUSD time and animated values: time samples, interpolation, clips, and stage-time mapping.
  • KHR_mesh_quantization, EXT_meshopt_compression, and KHR_texture_transform: mesh attribute storage, geometry compression, animation buffer compression, and UV transform math.
  • MikkTSpace: tangent-space consistency for normal maps and asset import/export.
  • Recast Navigation and Detour: navmesh construction/query, tiled navmeshes, and path query helpers.
  • PhysX geometry queries, NVIDIA PhysX SDK, and Jolt Physics: raycast, overlap, sweep, penetration, collision shape, and constraint math references.
  • CSS Values and Units, CSS Typed OM, W3C Geometry Interfaces, and CSSOM View: UI unit math, typed CSS values, DOMPoint, DOMRect, DOMQuad, DOMMatrix, scroll, and VisualViewport interop.
  • HTML canvas element: ImageData pixel manipulation, canvas color space, color type, device-pixel readback, and backing-store context options.
  • CSS Color 4, CSS Color 5, and CSS Color HDR: UI color spaces, color functions, contrast, and HDR output categories.
  • W3C WebNN API, ONNX broadcasting, and ONNX operators: tensor shapes, broadcasting, operator validation, and graph/tensor metadata.
  • WebAssembly Core 2.0 and WebAssembly vector instructions: SIMD lane-vector and v128 operation categories.
  • W3C WebRTC Stats, W3C WebTransport, RFC 3550 RTP, and RFC 9002 QUIC loss/congestion: real-time transport quality, jitter, packet loss, datagrams, and congestion math.

Audit Process References

  • Wiz code auditing guide: audit findings should be remediated by severity and verified with evidence.
  • Virtuoso QA root-cause analysis overview: RCA distinguishes genuine defects, environment issues, and test implementation problems.
  • Google Testing Blog: fast feedback loops support faster fixes and should complement larger end-to-end tests.
  • Konfirmity secure SDLC overview: findings registers should track severity, planned remediation, and closure evidence.
